ctcdcd on leave for 15 day, from 3-1-83 to 17-1-83 
and lor live days hum 28-2-83 lu 4-3-83, he was en¬ 
gaged on temporary basis. Thus he was taken work 
Jen 41 days as peon and sweeper intermittently and 
he has duly been paid the wages due lor all those 
days. As such, he is not eligible to any rebel. The 
additional submission of the Bank is such that the 
said workman had liled a suit in the Civil Court at 
Visavudar for wages for 271 days. In the said suit, 
tiie Judge came lo decide that the said workman 
had worked for three more days which he has not 
been paid wages for those three days; therefore, he 
should be paid wages for three days. Accordingly, the 
Bank had duly paid him wages for three days also. 
Being aggrieved by the said order, the said workman 
had liled an appeal in the District court; but he had 
withdrawn the said appeal. In these circumstances, 
the demand of the said workman that lie has not 
paid wages, cannot be accepted. 

5. The said workman has examined himself in 
support of Iiis submission at Exh, 17. The first 
party. Bank has not submitted any oral evidence., 
hut has submitted certain documentary evidence with 
The list of Exh. 10, 

ft. The submission of Shri Thesia, Advocate for 
the second party, is such that the said workman 
had worked continuously as Peon on temporary basis 
from 20-11-81 to 31-8-82. As such, before termi¬ 
nating him. the Bank ought to have given him one 
month's notice or wages for a month in lieu and 
retrenchment compensation, under Section 25-F of 
the Act of 1947. But the Bank has not issued notice 
according nor has paid compensation also. Therefore 
the order to terminate the workman is illegal. There¬ 
fore, decision Tumid be laken that be continues in 
service and he should be reinstated in service with 
full back wages. Against this, the submission of 
Shri Patel is such that the said workman has not 
worked continuously from 20-11-81 to 31-8-82; but 
has worked for 41 days intermittently on temporary 
hasis as peon oi sweeper. when people proceeded on 
leave, as stated by the Bank. As such, he is not 
eligible for anv compensation under Section 25-F or 
for anv notice before termination. Therefore, the 
said workman is not eligible for any relief. 

7. As a result, the Question to b e decided is, for 
bow many days the mid workman has worked in the 
Visavadar branch of the first party. Bank, and on 
what post did lie work ? The said workman has 
submitted ihnt he had worked continuously as neon 
from 20-11-81 to 31-8-82. But this submission 
made bv him appears »o he far from truth. The said 
workman accents in his cross-examination that in the 
Bank Smt. Barot war working as a Sweeper and 
Shri Padia war working as a Peon; and when thev 
proceeded on leave, he was encaged to work in their 
places: and wages for the said neriod has already 
been naid m Mm. But it is the additional submission 
of Shri Dhi r aikal that even on other days then when 
there two individuals' have been on leave, he has 
ennfinimnrlv worked, fm which he has not keen naid 
anv wanes This Mihmissinn made bv him is not 
acceptable became h- accents that with the then 
Bank Manager Shri Vaishnnv and 5 'bri Vnrn, who 
worked later he bad anv tusseh Tf the said work¬ 
man has worked for such a neriod in the Bank as 


taso/m^w a, lai 2 


a Peon, there is no reason tor these two individuals 
ior not paying to him the wages for that period. It 
is necessaiy to take note here that he has worked 
tor such a long time with the Bank and the Bank 
has not paid wages. As such to get the said wages 
the said workman had filed a suit in the Civil Court at 
Visavadar; but in the said suit the Bank was ordered 
for the wages of three more days and his submission 
that he had worked for 285 was not accepted. 
Against this decision he had raised a dispute before 
the Civil Com t but he had withdrawn the said dis¬ 
pute, In these circumstances, the submission of the 
said workman that he has worked in this Bank for 
such a long tim e and he has not been paid wages for 
the said period is not at all reliable. It seems that 
he has brought this fact simply to be reinstated in 
the Bank and is tar from truth. The said workman 
has not produced any other evidence to establish that 
he has worked for such a long period. The submis¬ 
sion that the Bank has not paid wages although he 
worked for such a long period' s not acceptable. The 
Bank Officers have nol to defray any amount from 
his salary or from his pocket. In such circumstances, 
it is not acceptable that the Bank Officers nray nol 
pay the wages to the said workman if they engage 
him. As a result, (hr. submission of Shri Dhirajlal 
that h>' has worked for rich a long period in the 
Bank does not <cem to have any weight. There is 
reason to believe that in view of the fact that the 
said workman was bemg engaged on temporary basis 
when the permanent Peon. Shri Padia and perma¬ 
nent Sweeper, Smt. Barot of the Bank proceeded on 
leave, to derive benefit of those circumstances, the 
said workman has bronchi out present demand. As a 
result, it does not appear that the <?*ud workman has 
worked continuously even on temporary basis in the 
Bank. In these circumstances, it is not necessary to 
issue any notice to him before termination: nor is it 
necessmv to nav him anv retrenchment comncnsn- 
tion. Recau te be has worked for less than 50 da vs 
and lhat too during the course of two-three years. 
As a result, th P -ubmission of the workman that he 
has worked continuously in the Bank from 20-11-81 
to 31-8-82 does not appear to be a fart and. there¬ 
fore, be is not entitled to anv relief. It cannot be 
'aid that the Bank has infringed anv provision of 
the Act of 1947. in not continuing the applicant in 
service. As a result, since the said workman is- not 
entillr 1 to anv relief, the following order is being 
passed in this reference : 

ORDER 

8 . There nnocars to be no truth in the demand 
ma.de concerning the 'aid workman; therefore, the 
c nid demand is not accepted. Taking into consi¬ 
deration the special circumstances, it does not ap¬ 
pear to be necessary lo pass any order concerning 
expenses.
